02:25:15 --> 02:25:19
in this video I want to share my tactic

02:25:16 --> 02:25:21
for dealing with motivation issues and

02:25:18 --> 02:25:23
part of that is the phrase that

02:25:20 --> 02:25:24
motivation is a myth now I've talked

02:25:22 --> 02:25:25
about this a few times in a couple of

02:25:23 --> 02:25:27
different videos and like podcasts and

02:25:24 --> 02:25:29
stuff but I haven't really fully

02:25:26 --> 02:25:31
explained it and the reason motivation

02:25:28 --> 02:25:33
is a myth is because motivation like

02:25:30 --> 02:25:36
what is motivation

02:25:32 --> 02:25:37
we only need motivation to do the things

02:25:35 --> 02:25:39
that we don't want to do and usually

02:25:36 --> 02:25:41
those are things that require a

02:25:38 --> 02:25:43
short-term pain in order for me to get

02:25:40 --> 02:25:45
long-term gain like we never say I need

02:25:42 --> 02:25:47
motivation to sit down and watch Netflix

02:25:44 --> 02:25:49
or I need motivation to hang out with my

02:25:46 --> 02:25:51
friends we say I need motivation to work

02:25:48 --> 02:25:53
and I need motivation to go to the gym

02:25:50 --> 02:25:54
because we know that those are not very

02:25:52 --> 02:25:57
pleasant in the short term but actually

02:25:53 --> 02:25:58
lead to good results in the long term

02:25:56 --> 02:26:00
um and the way our human psychology

02:25:57 --> 02:26:02
works is that we tend to very much

02:25:59 --> 02:26:03
overweight short-term benefits rather

02:26:01 --> 02:26:05
than long-term benefits because

02:26:02 --> 02:26:06
specially if it's like oh anything to

02:26:04 --> 02:26:09
do well in my exam so that I can get a

02:26:05 --> 02:26:11
job in 10 years time that's far too long

02:26:08 --> 02:26:13
term to actually be a reliable method of

02:26:10 --> 02:26:15
motivating Us in the present so why is

02:26:12 --> 02:26:18
motivation a myth well I think the gold

02:26:14 --> 02:26:20
standard approach is to just scrub the

02:26:17 --> 02:26:21
word motivation from our vocabulary and

02:26:19 --> 02:26:24
replace the concept of motivation with

02:26:20 --> 02:26:26
e concept of discipline so

02:26:23 --> 02:26:27
um there's an article on the website

02:26:25 --> 02:26:30
called Wiz domination which I'll link in

02:26:26 --> 02:26:32
the shown a bit of this uh this class

02:26:29 --> 02:26:34
which basically says that screw

02:26:31 --> 02:26:36
motivation what you need is discipline

02:26:33 --> 02:26:38
and they argue that you know there's the

02:26:35 --> 02:26:40
thought of I should do this thing

02:26:37 --> 02:26:41
and then there's the action of doing the

02:26:39 --> 02:26:43
thing itself and motivation kind of lies

02:26:40 --> 02:26:44
in the middle it's like like we have the

02:26:42 --> 02:26:46
thought I should do this thing

02:26:43 --> 02:26:48
motivation is when we say I need to feel

02:26:45 --> 02:26:50
ike doing this thing and the action is

02:26:47 --> 02:26:51
then just doing the thing itself so what

02:26:49 --> 02:26:53
his article argues and what I

02:26:51 --> 02:26:56
completely agree with is that there is

02:26:52 --> 02:26:58
absolutely no need for us to feel like

02:26:55 --> 02:27:00
doing something before we do the thing

02:26:57 --> 02:27:01
like you know this morning filming this

02:26:59 --> 02:27:03
class I didn't feel like filming this

02:27:00 --> 02:27:05
class but I did it anyway because I

02:27:02 --> 02:27:07
recognized that the way I feel is

02:27:04 --> 02:27:09
completely inconsequential to whether I

02:27:06 --> 02:27:11
do the thing or not at all and there's a

02:27:08 --> 02:27:12
few other bits in the article that are

02:27:10 --> 02:27:14
quite nice it says that um I think at

02:27:11 --> 02:27:16
one point in the article they say that a

02:27:13 --> 02:27:18
three-year-old bases its decisions on

02:27:15 --> 02:27:20
what it feels like an adult bases its

02:27:18 --> 02:27:22
decisions on what it knows it needs to

02:27:19 --> 02:27:24
be doing and so today when filming this

02:27:21 --> 02:27:25
class I was trying to be an adult but

02:27:23 --> 02:27:26
you know what I'm going to sit down and

02:27:24 --> 02:27:28
I'm going to do it as opposed to oh I

02:27:25 --> 02:27:30
need to get myself to feel like doing it

02:27:27 --> 02:27:32
first so the idea of motivation is this

02:27:30 --> 02:27:34
sort of infantile fantasy that we

02:27:31 --> 02:27:35
need to feel like doing something before

02:27:33 --> 02:27:37
we actually do it and in fact we can

02:27:34 --> 02:27:38
just operate discipline and just do the

02:27:36 --> 02:27:41
thing anyway that's kind of the gold

02:27:37 --> 02:27:42
standard um realistically you know I

and you can like apply it to your own

motivation for stuff we need motivation

02:28:22 --> 02:28:29
for Stuff where the short-term uh sort

02:28:26 --> 02:28:32
of short term it's not very pleasant and

02:28:28 --> 02:28:34
usually where the consequences for not

02:28:31 --> 02:28:37
doing the thing are not very great so

02:28:33 --> 02:28:39
for example if I if I told you you know

I would give you a million pounds to sit

down and do your homework you and Johnny

will sit down and do your homework like

it's not hard if I told you that I if

you don't do your homework you're gonna

02:28:45 --> 02:28:49
die you would Jolly will sit down and do

02:28:46 --> 02:28:50
your homework but usually we don't

02:28:48 --> 02:28:52
operate within those extremes of

02:28:49 --> 02:28:54
motivation because there's no need for

02:28:51 --> 02:28:57
motivation there because in one in one

02:28:53 --> 02:28:58
Circumstance the situation the outcome

02:28:56 --> 02:29:00
is so pleasurable in the here and now

02:28:57 --> 02:29:01
that we're going to do the work and in

02:28:59 --> 02:29:03
the other one the punishment for not

02:29:00 --> 02:29:04
doing the thing is so great that we're

02:29:02 --> 02:29:06
going to do the thing and those are the

02:29:03 --> 02:29:08
two elements of it so it's like how

02:29:05 --> 02:29:11
pleasurable is this thing to do versus

02:29:07 --> 02:29:13
how punishing is the is the alternative

02:29:10 --> 02:29:15
of not doing it and those are the

02:29:12 --> 02:29:17
domains that I think we can Target when

02:29:14 --> 02:29:18
it comes to motivation so the way that

02:29:16 --> 02:29:20
his works for me is that when I sit

02:29:17 --> 02:29:22
down to study I want it to be a pleasant

02:29:36 --> 02:29:40
less unpleasant with enough of these

hat you're going to lose 10 kilograms

onate it to charity even donated to the

thing I think step one is to recognize

my body that I'm I'm screwing up I'm

quite happy to get a takeaway like I

02:31:20 --> 02:31:24
ordered pizza today rather than cook

02:31:21 --> 02:31:26
something healthy because it's only my

02:31:23 --> 02:31:28
body and I don't know why that is but

02:31:25 --> 02:31:29
anyway kind of the the Top Line gold

02:31:27 --> 02:31:30
standard is that motivation is a myth

02:31:28 --> 02:31:33
and that we should scrub the word

02:31:29 --> 02:31:35
motivation from our vocabulary but you

02:31:32 --> 02:31:36
know we can recognize that that is an

02:31:34 --> 02:31:38
ideal that most of us don't attain

02:31:35 --> 02:31:40
cluding me and so we can then try and

02:31:37 --> 02:31:42
make the thing more pleasurable or try

02:31:39 --> 02:31:43
and make the alternative more unpleasant

02:31:41 --> 02:31:45
now there is a good book called the

02:31:42 --> 02:31:46
motivation myth which you can read if

02:31:44 --> 02:31:47
you like

02:31:45 --> 02:31:48
that talks about this in a bit more

02:31:46 --> 02:31:50
detail and I'll link to this Wiz

02:31:47 --> 02:31:51
domination article like that sort of

02:31:49 --> 02:31:53
changed the game for me when it came to

02:31:50 --> 02:31:55
thinking about motivation as being a

02:31:52 --> 02:31:57
myth and discipline as being the thing

02:31:54 --> 02:31:59
all right so we have plucked up

02:31:56 --> 02:32:01
officially the motivation or rather the

02:31:58 --> 02:32:03
discipline to sit down and do the

02:32:00 --> 02:32:05
studying now how do we not get

02:32:02 --> 02:32:07
distracted by our phone by the Internet

02:32:04 --> 02:32:08
by something else you know a bird flying

02:32:06 --> 02:32:10
through the window how do we not get

02:32:07 --> 02:32:12
distracted while doing artwork well

02:32:09 --> 02:32:13
firstly I think that kind of comes from

02:32:11 --> 02:32:16
enjoying the thing that we're doing

02:32:12 --> 02:32:17
usually again like you know we tend not

02:32:15 --> 02:32:18
o get distracted from watching Netflix

02:32:16 --> 02:32:20
we tend not to get distracted from

02:32:18 --> 02:32:21
hanging out with our friends or playing

02:32:19 --> 02:32:23
sports we tend to get distracted from

02:32:20 --> 02:32:25
doing work why is that often because

02:32:22 --> 02:32:27
it's not that fun and like sometimes for

02:32:24 --> 02:32:28
me when I'm in that real like Flow State

02:32:26 --> 02:32:29
where I'm really kind of enjoying what

02:32:27 --> 02:32:31
I'm doing I look up and look at the time

02:32:28 --> 02:32:32
I'm like damn an hour has passed and I

02:32:30 --> 02:32:33
just never noticed it and I didn't even

02:32:31 --> 02:32:35
think about getting distracted because I

02:32:32 --> 02:32:37
was really enjoying what I was doing and

02:32:34 --> 02:32:38
I was fully immersed in it so I think

02:32:36 --> 02:32:40
that's Point number one like if you can

02:32:37 --> 02:32:41
enjoy your subject and and even if you

02:32:39 --> 02:32:43
don't enjoy it if you can like really

02:32:40 --> 02:32:45
fully focus on it there's a book by Cal

02:32:42 --> 02:32:46
Newport called a deep work and he talks

02:32:44 --> 02:32:49
about this idea of like really deep

02:32:45 --> 02:32:51
intense focus on something that level of

02:32:48 --> 02:32:53
ocus this flow state is generally very

02:32:50 --> 02:32:54
pleasurable says the evidence about the

02:32:52 --> 02:32:56
stuff so that means we're less likely to

02:32:53 --> 02:32:58
be distracted the other the other

02:32:55 --> 02:33:00
culprits of things that distract us are

02:32:57 --> 02:33:02
firstly are phone so what I do if I'm

02:32:59 --> 02:33:03
studying is I would turn my phone to do

02:33:01 --> 02:33:06
not disturb and I would put it face down

02:33:02 --> 02:33:07
on the table in front of me or in my bag

02:33:05 --> 02:33:09
or even sometimes if I'm really

02:33:06 --> 02:33:11
desperate I would put it in a different

02:33:08 --> 02:33:12
room and yeah sometimes people say oh

02:33:10 --> 02:33:14
what if there's an emergency but like

02:33:12 --> 02:33:15
realistically when was the last time you

02:33:13 --> 02:33:17
had an emergency that you genuinely

02:33:14 --> 02:33:18
needed to respond to on your phone and

02:33:16 --> 02:33:20
if there is an emergency the Do Not

02:33:17 --> 02:33:23
Disturb setting I think lets people call

02:33:19 --> 02:33:24
you if they ring twice in a row or you

02:33:22 --> 02:33:26
can set up like safe people that you

02:33:23 --> 02:33:27
know if my if mother calls me or if my

02:33:25 --> 02:33:29
kid calls me then I'll then the phone

02:33:26 --> 02:33:31
will ring but if anyone else messages

02:33:28 --> 02:33:33
then it won't so that's another super

02:33:30 --> 02:33:34
easy way it was a super easy thing to do

02:33:32 --> 02:33:36
because even even to this day I find

02:33:33 --> 02:33:37
myself scrolling through Instagram and

02:33:35 --> 02:33:39
being like Oh how many likes have I got

02:33:36 --> 02:33:41
on that pick uh whereas if the phone is

02:33:38 --> 02:33:43
face down on silent mode or in a

02:33:40 --> 02:33:45
different room entirely then I just

02:33:42 --> 02:33:46
don't think about it I just kind of do

02:33:44 --> 02:33:47
my thing there's a few other things we

02:33:45 --> 02:33:49
can do with the phone

02:33:46 --> 02:33:51
um we can increase the amount of

02:33:48 --> 02:33:53
riction that it takes to do bad things

02:33:50 --> 02:33:54
on our third so for example what my

02:33:52 --> 02:33:56
brother does is that he uninstalls

02:33:54 --> 02:33:58
Instagram the the app from his phone so

02:33:55 --> 02:34:00
in order to go on Instagram he has to go

02:33:57 --> 02:34:02
n safaric on instagram.com log in and

02:33:59 --> 02:34:04
then he's on Instagram and those added

02:34:01 --> 02:34:05
few steps mean that he's significantly

02:34:03 --> 02:34:07
less likely to actually go on Instagram

02:34:04 --> 02:34:09
well another friend of mine does is that

02:34:06 --> 02:34:11
she buries her social media apps in like

02:34:08 --> 02:34:12
some random folder on like page 8 of the

02:34:10 --> 02:34:14
iPhone so in order to get there she has

02:34:12 --> 02:34:16
to like Scrolls Scrolls scroll page a

02:34:13 --> 02:34:18
click on the folder and then click on

02:34:15 --> 02:34:19
Instagram and that supposedly is enough

02:34:17 --> 02:34:22
friction to make a knock on Instagram

02:34:18 --> 02:34:23
and in fact like that that friction is

02:34:21 --> 02:34:25
great because like while we're while

02:34:22 --> 02:34:26
we're doing the thing it just gives our

02:34:24 --> 02:34:28
brain a chance to think you know do I

02:34:25 --> 02:34:30
really want to be doing this is this

02:34:27 --> 02:34:31
really a good use of my time

02:34:29 --> 02:34:32
um and sort of on that note one

02:34:30 --> 02:34:34
technique that I found that's completely

02:34:31 --> 02:34:35
transformed my sleep is the fact that I

02:34:33 --> 02:34:38
now put the phone across the room from

02:34:34 --> 02:34:39
e and on days I find where I'm like oh

02:34:37 --> 02:34:41
you know I'll just go on my phone for

02:34:38 --> 02:34:42
five minutes I end up staying up why is

02:34:40 --> 02:34:44
this off I end up staying up for like 20

02:34:41 --> 02:34:46
minutes so like even two hours sometimes

02:34:43 --> 02:34:48
when I've got work the next morning at

02:34:45 --> 02:34:49
6am and I'm staying up for an extra two

02:34:47 --> 02:34:50
hours until 3am just scrolling through

02:34:48 --> 02:34:52
Instagram on my phone and kind of hating

02:34:49 --> 02:34:53
myself for doing it but on the days

02:34:51 --> 02:34:55
where I leave the phone on the bedside

02:34:52 --> 02:34:57
you know across the room on the chest of

02:34:54 --> 02:34:58
drawers I don't get distracted from it

02:34:56 --> 02:34:59
and then I get a much better night's

02:34:57 --> 02:35:00
leep and I'm much happier for it

02:34:58 --> 02:35:01
here's a few other things you can do

02:34:59 --> 02:35:03
you know there's all these apps that

02:35:00 --> 02:35:05
block apps from your phone like from

02:35:02 --> 02:35:07
your computer you know self-control and

02:35:04 --> 02:35:08
stuff blocks Facebook and you know

02:35:06 --> 02:35:09
there's all these Chrome extensions to

02:35:07 --> 02:35:11
remove your Facebook feed if that's what

02:35:08 --> 02:35:14
distracts you another thing you can do

02:35:10 --> 02:35:15
is you can set your phone to grayscale

02:35:13 --> 02:35:17
um so you know if you just Google I

02:35:14 --> 02:35:19
think on on iPhone you can do it in the

02:35:16 --> 02:35:20
accessibility options and display

02:35:18 --> 02:35:22
options so it just removes all the color

02:35:19 --> 02:35:24
from your phone and kind of seeing your

02:35:21 --> 02:35:25
phone in grayscale just to be like oh

02:35:23 --> 02:35:27
that's a bit grim and then it just makes

02:35:24 --> 02:35:28
us less likely to want to go on our

02:35:26 --> 02:35:29
phone because it's in grayscale mode

02:35:27 --> 02:35:31
that's another thing you can do but all

02:35:28 --> 02:35:33
of these are basically hacks to try and

02:35:30 --> 02:35:35
help us Focus whereas I think that the

02:35:32 --> 02:35:37
main thing is to just be able to enjoy

02:35:34 --> 02:35:38
the subject and to focus intently on it

02:35:36 --> 02:35:40
because if we're doing that then it

02:35:37 --> 02:35:41
becomes fun and then we don't feel the

02:35:39 --> 02:35:43
need to get distracted by all these

02:35:40 --> 02:35:45
other bits and bobs

02:35:42 --> 02:35:47
all right so we have developed the

02:35:44 --> 02:35:49
motivational the discipline to sit down

02:35:46 --> 02:35:50
and do our work we have

02:35:48 --> 02:35:52
um been able to not get distracted by

02:35:49 --> 02:35:54
using our phone and now we're focusing

02:35:51 --> 02:35:56
for an extended period of time but we

02:35:53 --> 02:35:57
probably want to be taking breaks during

02:35:55 --> 02:36:00
that during that focusing session

02:35:57 --> 02:36:01
because there is some evidence I've got

02:35:59 --> 02:36:02
a research paper here that says we

02:36:00 --> 02:36:04
propose that deactivating and

02:36:01 --> 02:36:06
reactivating your goals allows you to

02:36:03 --> 02:36:08
stay focused from a practical standpoint

02:36:05 --> 02:36:09
of research suggests that when faced

02:36:07 --> 02:36:12
with long tasks such as studying before

02:36:09 --> 02:36:14
a final exam or doing your taxes it is

02:36:11 --> 02:36:16
best to impose brief breaks on yourself

02:36:13 --> 02:36:18
brief mental breaks will actually help

02:36:15 --> 02:36:20
you stay focused on your task so

02:36:17 --> 02:36:21
basically we should be taking breaks

02:36:19 --> 02:36:23
while we're studying but we should be

02:36:20 --> 02:36:24
intentional about the breaks that we're

02:36:22 --> 02:36:25
taking and one of the most popular

02:36:23 --> 02:36:27
methods for doing this is called the

02:36:24 --> 02:36:31
Pomodoro Technique and that was invented

02:36:26 --> 02:36:33
by a guy called Francesco Cirillo in the

02:36:30 --> 02:36:35
1990s and he named it after the timer he

02:36:32 --> 02:36:37
used to use a tomato timer to time his

02:36:34 --> 02:36:38
Pomodoro sessions and that's why it's

02:36:36 --> 02:36:40
called the Pomodoro Technique or the

02:36:37 --> 02:36:42
Tomato technique and the idea behind the

02:36:39 --> 02:36:44
Pomodoro Technique is that you work for

02:36:41 --> 02:36:46
25 minutes then you you have a five

02:36:43 --> 02:36:48
minute break and you work for 25 minutes

02:36:45 --> 02:36:49
have a five minute break work for 25

02:36:47 --> 02:36:51
minutes five minute break well for 25

02:36:48 --> 02:36:52
minutes five minute break then after

02:36:50 --> 02:36:54
doing four work sessions and a five

02:36:51 --> 02:36:56
minute break you then take an extended

02:36:53 --> 02:36:57
half an hour break or 20 minute break or

02:36:55 --> 02:36:59
something like that and then you repeat

02:36:56 --> 02:37:02
he process and the idea is that that 25

02:36:58 --> 02:37:04
minute timer is enough to give us sort

02:37:01 --> 02:37:06
of ample time to focus on the thing but

02:37:03 --> 02:37:09
it also means that we have a regular

02:37:05 --> 02:37:11
break so that you know when we're never

02:37:08 --> 02:37:14
gonna kind of get tired of focusing as

02:37:10 --> 02:37:15
uch and it it's it's quite a nice sort

02:37:13 --> 02:37:18
of rigid structure that we can impose on

02:37:14 --> 02:37:19
ourselves to help us maintain Focus for

02:37:17 --> 02:37:21
longer there's a few pros and a few cons

02:37:18 --> 02:37:23
of this and I don't really use the

02:37:20 --> 02:37:25
Pomodoro Technique in that sort of

02:37:22 --> 02:37:27
version of it anymore I used to when I

02:37:24 --> 02:37:28
was in my first and second year and the

02:37:26 --> 02:37:30
reason I used to use it is because it's

02:37:27 --> 02:37:31
quite a social thing like I would go to

02:37:29 --> 02:37:33
the library with my friends and we would

02:37:30 --> 02:37:36
all collectively pump together we called

02:37:32 --> 02:37:38
it plumbing and so we would start a pom

02:37:35 --> 02:37:40
you know with like a a tap on the on the

02:37:37 --> 02:37:42
desk and then we would end the Palm with

02:37:39 --> 02:37:44
a tap on the desk and then we would end

02:37:41 --> 02:37:45
the five minute break with two taps and

02:37:43 --> 02:37:47
that would be a sign that the next

02:37:44 --> 02:37:48
problem is going to begin and it was

02:37:46 --> 02:37:49
just quite a fun like social thing

02:37:47 --> 02:37:51
because everyone could be doing

02:37:48 --> 02:37:53
different subjects and in fact we were

02:37:50 --> 02:37:54
we all were doing different subjects but

02:37:52 --> 02:37:56
we were all in the same library at my

02:37:53 --> 02:37:57
college Emmanuel College

02:37:55 --> 02:38:00
um and we would kind of pump together

02:37:56 --> 02:38:01
and it would feel like a sort of shared

02:37:59 --> 02:38:02
community group learning session even

02:38:00 --> 02:38:04
though we were all doing different

02:38:01 --> 02:38:06
subjects so that's one of the advantages

02:38:03 --> 02:38:08
the other Advantage is that if you like

02:38:05 --> 02:38:09
if we find it hard to focus on the thing

02:38:07 --> 02:38:12
or what we're doing is really isn't fun

02:38:08 --> 02:38:14
like doing our taxes or whatever then

02:38:11 --> 02:38:16
that at the end of that 25 minute

02:38:13 --> 02:38:17
session is sort of like a light at the

02:38:15 --> 02:38:18
nd of the tunnel and we can think oh

02:38:16 --> 02:38:20
yes you know I've only got seven more

02:38:17 --> 02:38:21
minutes to focus and then we can focus

02:38:19 --> 02:38:23
for seven minutes and then we can

02:38:20 --> 02:38:25
breathe a sigh of relief as we take that

02:38:22 --> 02:38:26
break so that's sort of the the benefits

02:38:24 --> 02:38:29
of the Pomodoro Technique and of course

02:38:25 --> 02:38:30
it forces us to take breaks so it's good

02:38:28 --> 02:38:32
um the reason I don't really use it

02:38:29 --> 02:38:35
anymore in that in that particular realm

02:38:31 --> 02:38:37
is that usually when I'm studying I

02:38:34 --> 02:38:38
don't have a problem with focusing for

02:38:36 --> 02:38:40
more than 25 minutes if I'm doing

02:38:37 --> 02:38:41
something that's really boring then I do

02:38:39 --> 02:38:43
but most of the stuff that I do in my

02:38:40 --> 02:38:45
life is just generally quite fun and if

02:38:42 --> 02:38:47
I'm studying renal physiology or

02:38:44 --> 02:38:49
preparing for an exam or something then

02:38:46 --> 02:38:51
I actually kind of focus for about 40 or

02:38:48 --> 02:38:52
45 minutes before I can sort of

02:38:50 --> 02:38:54
internally just sense myself losing

02:38:51 --> 02:38:56
focus and at that point I'm like okay

02:38:53 --> 02:38:58
cool it's been 45 minutes I'm gonna take

02:38:55 --> 02:38:59
a 15 minute break and make myself a cup

02:38:57 --> 02:39:01
of coffee or something like that I think

02:38:58 --> 02:39:03
the Pomodoro Technique does have its

02:39:00 --> 02:39:05
benefits but I don't think we should we

02:39:02 --> 02:39:07
shouldn't like stick to it so rigidly

02:39:04 --> 02:39:09
like we should use a technique as as a

02:39:06 --> 02:39:11
tool rather than being a slave to the

02:39:08 --> 02:39:12
technique and I know lots of people who

02:39:10 --> 02:39:14
say things like oh I tried the

02:39:11 --> 02:39:15
Pomodoro Technique but I got really

02:39:13 --> 02:39:17
annoyed when the 25 minutes was up

02:39:14 --> 02:39:18
because I actually needed an extra five

02:39:16 --> 02:39:19
minutes you know if you need an extra

02:39:17 --> 02:39:21
five minutes to finish the bit that

02:39:18 --> 02:39:23
you're doing take the extra five minutes

02:39:20 --> 02:39:24
like this is just a guideline it's not

02:39:22 --> 02:39:26
really a rule sort of like the pirate's

02:39:24 --> 02:39:27
code in Pirates of the Caribbean

02:39:25 --> 02:39:29
um but yeah that's what the Pomodoro

02:39:26 --> 02:39:30
method is useful for and so many people

02:39:28 --> 02:39:32
swear by it there's a few different apps

02:39:30 --> 02:39:34
that you can use for it

02:39:31 --> 02:39:36
um I think if you just type in Pomodoro

02:39:33 --> 02:39:37
Timer on Google it's got one built in so

02:39:35 --> 02:39:40
we wish to use that one there's a

02:39:36 --> 02:39:42
website tomatotimer.com that also works

02:39:39 --> 02:39:43
and then there's the app that I used to

02:39:41 --> 02:39:45
use for this when I was in my fifth year

02:39:42 --> 02:39:47
of med school and that app is called

02:39:44 --> 02:39:49
forest and the idea behind Forest is

02:39:46 --> 02:39:50
that you start your pom session and uh

02:39:48 --> 02:39:52
the way it works is that it kind of

02:39:49 --> 02:39:54
grows a tree for you and if you go off

02:39:51 --> 02:39:56
the app or if you go to a different app

02:39:53 --> 02:39:57
on your phone during that 25 minutes

02:39:55 --> 02:40:00
then your tree dies and you have to

02:39:57 --> 02:40:01
start again so as your tree is growing

02:39:59 --> 02:40:03
you have to kind of stay on the app

02:40:00 --> 02:40:04
screen watching the tree grow for 25

02:40:02 --> 02:40:06
minutes which forces you to not get

02:40:03 --> 02:40:08
distracted with your phone and then the

02:40:05 --> 02:40:10
five minute break kind of times it for

02:40:07 --> 02:40:12
you and then it starts a new tree

02:40:09 --> 02:40:14
there's a quite a nice way of pomodoring

02:40:11 --> 02:40:15
or Plumbing because you know that you

02:40:13 --> 02:40:17
can't go on your phone because then your

02:40:14 --> 02:40:18
tree is going to die

02:40:16 --> 02:40:19
um and they've got like a leaderboard

02:40:17 --> 02:40:20
and you can like connect with your

02:40:18 --> 02:40:22
friends and stuff so you can see who's

02:40:19 --> 02:40:24
doing how many Pomodoro sessions in a

02:40:21 --> 02:40:25
given day and you can be like oh damn

02:40:23 --> 02:40:26
Marcus did eight hours worth of pumps

02:40:24 --> 02:40:28
yesterday he must be doing really well

02:40:25 --> 02:40:30
in exams why don't I you know motivate

02:40:27 --> 02:40:32
myself to do a bit more studying so that

02:40:30 --> 02:40:33
was how I used to use Pomodoro method in

02:40:31 --> 02:40:35
fifth year when preparing for my

02:40:32 --> 02:40:37
Obstetrics and Gynecology and Pediatrics

02:40:34 --> 02:40:39
exams but like I said I don't really use

02:40:36 --> 02:40:41
it as really rigidly anymore I think I'm

02:40:39 --> 02:40:43
now at the point where I can just kind

02:40:40 --> 02:40:45
of focus for 45 minutes 50 minutes at a

02:40:42 --> 02:40:47
time and then I'll take a break and yeah

02:40:44 --> 02:40:48
that's all good so that's the Pomodoro

02:40:46 --> 02:40:50
method

02:40:47 --> 02:40:52
all right so we've motivated ourselves

02:40:49 --> 02:40:53
to sit down and do the work we're not

02:40:51 --> 02:40:55
getting distracted we're doing the

02:40:52 --> 02:40:57
Pomodoro Technique

02:40:54 --> 02:40:59
but do we listen to music or not and

02:40:56 --> 02:41:00
that is the age-old question isn't it

02:40:58 --> 02:41:01
basically what the evidence shows and

02:40:59 --> 02:41:03
I'm gonna do like a proper YouTube video

02:41:00 --> 02:41:05
n this in a while but I'll give you a

02:41:02 --> 02:41:07
you know a heads up what the evidence

02:41:04 --> 02:41:09
shows is that music with lyrics is

02:41:06 --> 02:41:11
probably the worst because the idea is

02:41:08 --> 02:41:12
that the lyrics if they've got words in

02:41:10 --> 02:41:14
them they interfere with our working

02:41:11 --> 02:41:16
memory and the phonological loop which

02:41:13 --> 02:41:18
is sort of the kind of random access

02:41:15 --> 02:41:20
memory sort of the function within our

02:41:17 --> 02:41:21
working memory that processes words and

02:41:19 --> 02:41:23
if you've got lyrics in the background

02:41:20 --> 02:41:25
or in your ears and you're trying to

02:41:22 --> 02:41:27
read words at the same time that will

02:41:24 --> 02:41:29
cause interference and that's going to

02:41:26 --> 02:41:30
be bad so music with lyrics is generally

02:41:28 --> 02:41:31
considered a bad thing when you're

02:41:29 --> 02:41:34
trying to study

02:41:30 --> 02:41:37
music without lyrics so instrumental

02:41:33 --> 02:41:37
tracks are sort of a middle ground

02:41:36 --> 02:41:39
um

02:41:36 --> 02:41:41
there's a few different a few different

02:41:38 --> 02:41:43
heories about this um working in

02:41:40 --> 02:41:45
absolute silence is probably the most

02:41:42 --> 02:41:46
optimal uh because working in absolute

02:41:44 --> 02:41:48
silence means that we're not going to

02:41:45 --> 02:41:49
get distracted by the music firstly and

02:41:47 --> 02:41:51
there's this effect in memory which is

02:41:48 --> 02:41:54
that if you're studying for something

02:41:50 --> 02:41:56
like an exam in a similar environment to

02:41:53 --> 02:41:57
when you actually take the exam then

02:41:55 --> 02:41:59
your performance is going to be

02:41:56 --> 02:42:00
increased so for example when we take

02:41:58 --> 02:42:03
xams usually we're in a very quiet room

02:41:59 --> 02:42:04
surrounded by the rustling of papers and

02:42:02 --> 02:42:06
people in the sounds of people writing

02:42:03 --> 02:42:07
and the occasional cough but not much

02:42:05 --> 02:42:09
else going on there's certainly no

02:42:06 --> 02:42:10
background music unless you're in like

02:42:08 --> 02:42:12
the Senate house in Cambridge and

02:42:09 --> 02:42:14
there's someone busking outside outside

02:42:11 --> 02:42:15
the door there's no background music

02:42:13 --> 02:42:17
um there's no like lyrics in the

02:42:14 --> 02:42:19
background definitely

02:42:16 --> 02:42:21
basically Silence with like

02:42:18 --> 02:42:23
scratching of papers so basically the

02:42:20 --> 02:42:24
sort of sound environment that you would

02:42:22 --> 02:42:26
get in a library and they've done some

02:42:23 --> 02:42:27
studies where they've compared people

02:42:25 --> 02:42:30
who learn like a series of words

02:42:27 --> 02:42:32
underwater then they do better when

02:42:29 --> 02:42:33
they're tested underwater compared to

02:42:31 --> 02:42:34
people who learn it on land and then

02:42:32 --> 02:42:36
they do better when they're tested on

02:42:33 --> 02:42:38
land but if you learn words on land and

02:42:35 --> 02:42:39
you're tested underwater or you learn

02:42:37 --> 02:42:41
them underwater and you're tested on

02:42:38 --> 02:42:42
land then your performance gets reduced

02:42:40 --> 02:42:44
and the idea behind that is that our

02:42:41 --> 02:42:45
brain has to somehow has this

02:42:43 --> 02:42:47
association with this sort of

02:42:44 --> 02:42:49
environment that you're in when you

02:42:46 --> 02:42:50
learn something and the best way to

02:42:48 --> 02:42:52
recall the information is to mimic the

02:42:49 --> 02:42:53
nvironment as best as you can the

02:42:51 --> 02:42:55
vidence does suggest that we should

02:42:52 --> 02:42:56
probably be studying in silence but I'm

02:42:54 --> 02:42:57
personally going to ignore that and

02:42:55 --> 02:42:59
study with instrumentals which I think

02:42:56 --> 02:43:01
is a good middle ground between complete

02:42:58 --> 02:43:03
silence which is boring and music with

02:43:00 --> 02:43:05
lyrics which is incredibly distracting

02:43:02 --> 02:43:07
so hopefully that was helpful

02:43:04 --> 02:43:09
Okay so we've talked about the Pomodoro

02:43:06 --> 02:43:10
Technique which is all about taking a

02:43:08 --> 02:43:12
break uh sort of within your study

02:43:09 --> 02:43:14
sessions so 25 minutes of work and five

02:43:11 --> 02:43:15
minutes a break but in this video I just

02:43:13 --> 02:43:17
want to talk about some general tips for

02:43:14 --> 02:43:20
maintaining a healthy ish work-life

02:43:16 --> 02:43:21
balance while studying I think the most

02:43:19 --> 02:43:23
important thing is to realize that

02:43:20 --> 02:43:26
really like our physical and our mental

02:43:22 --> 02:43:28
health comes far above any sort of exam

02:43:25 --> 02:43:30
and stuff and yeah I get that sometimes

02:43:27 --> 02:43:32
we have high stakes exams but for the

02:43:29 --> 02:43:35
most part our exams really don't matter

02:43:31 --> 02:43:36
in the grand scheme of things especially

02:43:34 --> 02:43:37
if they're coming at the expense of a

02:43:35 --> 02:43:40
physical or mental health and I just

02:43:36 --> 02:43:42
find that that's something to to keep in

02:43:39 --> 02:43:44
the back of my mind because I've got

02:43:41 --> 02:43:47
so many friends who kind of

02:43:43 --> 02:43:48
burnt out because you know it's it's so

02:43:46 --> 02:43:49
easy when you're at University or you're

02:43:47 --> 02:43:51
in school and in the right environment

02:43:48 --> 02:43:53
or in the wrong environment rather to

02:43:50 --> 02:43:55
sort of everyone to sort of work each

02:43:52 --> 02:43:56
other up to the point where where we

02:43:54 --> 02:43:58
feel that oh my God this exam is

02:43:55 --> 02:43:59
literally the most important thing in my

02:43:57 --> 02:44:01
life and because everyone around us

02:43:58 --> 02:44:02
eems to be working then we'll kind of

02:44:00 --> 02:44:05
trying to kill ourselves working as well

02:44:01 --> 02:44:07
and I don't know it's just not an ideal

02:44:04 --> 02:44:09
way to go about it because you know as

02:44:06 --> 02:44:10
we're saying physical and mental health

02:44:08 --> 02:44:11
comes first secondly in terms of

02:44:09 --> 02:44:13
work-life balance

02:44:10 --> 02:44:15
um personally the thing that worked for

02:44:12 --> 02:44:17
me was that I would go to different

02:44:14 --> 02:44:19
places to do work and then I would have

02:44:16 --> 02:44:21
my room as like a work-free zone so for

02:44:18 --> 02:44:22
example when I was in university I'd be

02:44:20 --> 02:44:23
going to different libraries and

02:44:21 --> 02:44:25
studying and lived in in the different

02:44:22 --> 02:44:26
libraries but then when I'd come back

02:44:24 --> 02:44:28
home I'd sort of had a rule for myself

02:44:25 --> 02:44:30
that I'm not gonna I'm not gonna let

02:44:27 --> 02:44:31
myself study when I'm at home I would

02:44:29 --> 02:44:33
have to study outside the house and that

02:44:30 --> 02:44:36
was a very distinctive sort of work-life

02:44:32 --> 02:44:37
Separation but also sometimes what I

02:44:35 --> 02:44:39
found useful is to do more of a sort of

02:44:36 --> 02:44:41
work-life integration so especially when

02:44:38 --> 02:44:43
it comes to preparing for multiple

02:44:40 --> 02:44:44
choice exams where where the technique

02:44:42 --> 02:44:46
for preparing for those is to just bash

02:44:43 --> 02:44:48
through lots of questions on an online

02:44:45 --> 02:44:50
question bank I found that me and quite

02:44:47 --> 02:44:51
a few of my other friends um the

02:44:49 --> 02:44:53
technique that worked for us was that we

02:44:50 --> 02:44:55
just sort of casually be working and

02:44:52 --> 02:44:57
oing these questions as we're going

02:44:54 --> 02:44:58
about throughout the day so for example

02:44:56 --> 02:44:59
t's say we're all chilling in a room

02:44:57 --> 02:45:00
and we've just ordered some pizza and

02:44:58 --> 02:45:02
stuff some of us would be on our

02:44:59 --> 02:45:04
computers or our laptops kind of doing

02:45:01 --> 02:45:06
Anki flashcards or doing questions on

02:45:03 --> 02:45:08
past test or past master and while also

02:45:05 --> 02:45:10
kind of having a chat so that's a sort

02:45:07 --> 02:45:12
of more like it's not a very focused way

02:45:09 --> 02:45:13
of studying but it's a way of getting in

02:45:11 --> 02:45:15
the Reps of doing the flashcards of

02:45:12 --> 02:45:16
doing the questions while also hanging

02:45:14 --> 02:45:18
around with friends and kind of being a

02:45:15 --> 02:45:20
little bit sociable the other really

02:45:17 --> 02:45:22
important thing is to you know eat well

02:45:19 --> 02:45:24
and exercise and stuff like a mistake

02:45:21 --> 02:45:26
that I made uh during exam season was I

02:45:23 --> 02:45:27
thought oh you know you know in in exam

02:45:25 --> 02:45:29
season I need to sort of really just

02:45:26 --> 02:45:31
focus on studying and not actually go to

02:45:28 --> 02:45:33
the gym but I found that for the weeks

02:45:30 --> 02:45:35
where I did that I ended up personally

02:45:32 --> 02:45:36
feeling like less good about myself and

02:45:34 --> 02:45:39
that sort of bled into my studying

02:45:35 --> 02:45:40
whereas even today if you know I've got

02:45:38 --> 02:45:41
a whole day where I have to

02:45:39 --> 02:45:43
churn out a bunch of videos or prepare

02:45:40 --> 02:45:45
for a supervision I still make it a

02:45:42 --> 02:45:46
point that you know two or three times

02:45:44 --> 02:45:48
that week I'm going to go to the gym

02:45:45 --> 02:45:50
because I recognize that actually in the

02:45:48 --> 02:45:52
grand scheme of things you know my own

02:45:49 --> 02:45:54
physical health my own nutrition my own

02:45:51 --> 02:45:56
mental health and stuff all of that is

02:45:53 --> 02:45:57
far more important than you know doing a

02:45:55 --> 02:45:59
few more questions or preparing one more

02:45:56 --> 02:46:00
YouTube video or like preparing a few

02:45:58 --> 02:46:02
more slides for the supervision so I

02:45:59 --> 02:46:03
think the work-life balance thing is

02:46:01 --> 02:46:05
really just about recognizing that our

02:46:02 --> 02:46:07
priorities and recognize having that

02:46:04 --> 02:46:09
studying is not usually top of our

02:46:06 --> 02:46:11
priority list sure depending on the exam

02:46:08 --> 02:46:12
and some Heist takes exams it has to be

02:46:10 --> 02:46:14
but the other way I like to think of

02:46:11 --> 02:46:17
this is that it's okay for life to be

02:46:13 --> 02:46:18
out of balance for a short amount of

02:46:16 --> 02:46:20
time like we can have different seasons

02:46:17 --> 02:46:22
in our life where you know let's say it

02:46:19 --> 02:46:24
is the week before our exams

02:46:21 --> 02:46:25
it's probably reasonable to spend that

02:46:23 --> 02:46:27
week just full-on focused on cramming

02:46:24 --> 02:46:29
and and kind of neglecting our friends a

02:46:26 --> 02:46:30
little bit because it's just for a week

02:46:28 --> 02:46:32
it's just for a very short amount of

02:46:29 --> 02:46:34
time but I think like what if we're

02:46:31 --> 02:46:35
going that far out of balance we should

02:46:33 --> 02:46:38
really keep it in mind and try and

02:46:34 --> 02:46:39
redress the balance later on so yeah

02:46:37 --> 02:46:40
just a few General tips on work-life

02:46:38 --> 02:46:41
balance

02:46:39 --> 02:46:43
all right let's talk about one of my

02:46:40 --> 02:46:45
favorite topics and that is how to study

02:46:42 --> 02:46:47
effectively with friends now this was

02:46:44 --> 02:46:49
omething that I I was doing like from

02:46:46 --> 02:46:50
y first year of University onwards and

02:46:48 --> 02:46:52
I think it was one of the few things

02:46:49 --> 02:46:53
that really kind of changed the game in

02:46:51 --> 02:46:55
terms of making studying a lot more fun

02:46:52 --> 02:46:57
for me and there's a few tips I've got

02:46:54 --> 02:46:58
for studying with friends so firstly you

02:46:56 --> 02:47:00
want to find the right group of people

02:46:57 --> 02:47:01
who is all motivated to study at around

02:46:59 --> 02:47:03
about a similar level

02:47:00 --> 02:47:04
um there's not much Point getting people

02:47:02 --> 02:47:05
in the group who absolutely hate

02:47:03 --> 02:47:07
studying them who are going to distract

02:47:04 --> 02:47:09
everyone so you kind of want to have a

02:47:06 --> 02:47:10
group of friends who who are like you

02:47:08 --> 02:47:12
know a bit of a nerd themselves ideally

02:47:09 --> 02:47:14
secondly I think it's really important

02:47:11 --> 02:47:15
hat someone in the group takes charge

02:47:13 --> 02:47:17
of what the study session is going to

02:47:14 --> 02:47:18
look like so for me and my friends I was

02:47:16 --> 02:47:21
usually the one that took charge because

02:47:17 --> 02:47:23
you know someone's got to do it

02:47:20 --> 02:47:24
um and and and the way that we would do

02:47:22 --> 02:47:26
the session is we would all get together

02:47:23 --> 02:47:27
in the library of Emmanuel College and

02:47:25 --> 02:47:29
we'd be using the Pomodoro Technique so

02:47:26 --> 02:47:31
we'd have 25 minutes of work and then

02:47:28 --> 02:47:34
five minute break 25 minutes work five

02:47:30 --> 02:47:36
minute break and we would kind of tap on

02:47:33 --> 02:47:37
the table to Signal when these breaks

02:47:35 --> 02:47:38
were starting and finishing and we had

02:47:36 --> 02:47:40
one person who was like the Pomodoro

02:47:37 --> 02:47:42
master who would have the Pomodoro Timer

02:47:39 --> 02:47:43
up on their screen and who'd be sort of

02:47:41 --> 02:47:45
time keeping for everyone else in the

02:47:42 --> 02:47:47
group a few other pointers so if you're

02:47:44 --> 02:47:49
studying the same topic it can be very

02:47:46 --> 02:47:51
easy for you know one person to stop you

02:47:48 --> 02:47:52
know shouting out all the answers and

02:47:50 --> 02:47:54
the other people to just sort of be like

02:47:51 --> 02:47:56
oh yeah I knew that so one point that we

02:47:53 --> 02:47:59
would do is that whenever we we got

02:47:55 --> 02:48:00
multiple of our uh usmetics in the room

02:47:58 --> 02:48:02
together and if we if we were all

02:47:59 --> 02:48:04
studying the same thing collectively we

02:48:01 --> 02:48:06
would for example see a flashcard on the

02:48:03 --> 02:48:08
screen and then we would impose like a

02:48:05 --> 02:48:09
10 second silence where everyone had a

02:48:07 --> 02:48:11
chance to think about it and ideally

02:48:08 --> 02:48:12
write an answer down on paper so they

02:48:10 --> 02:48:14
committed themselves to paper whereas if

02:48:11 --> 02:48:16
it was just a free-for-all then you know

02:48:13 --> 02:48:17
Paul you know who knew the answer to

02:48:15 --> 02:48:18
everything would just shout out the

02:48:16 --> 02:48:20
answer and the rest of us wouldn't have

02:48:17 --> 02:48:21
a chance to actually use active recall

02:48:19 --> 02:48:23
to try and dredge up that information

02:48:20 --> 02:48:25
from our brains so the main thing I

02:48:22 --> 02:48:26
would say is that if you are doing the

02:48:24 --> 02:48:28
same thing make sure you give everyone

02:48:25 --> 02:48:30
in the group a chance to do the thing

02:48:27 --> 02:48:32
and if that requires imposing moments of

02:48:29 --> 02:48:35
Silence that's absolutely fine you might

02:48:31 --> 02:48:36
have to be a you know totalitarian

02:48:34 --> 02:48:38
Tyrant uh for a little while but

02:48:35 --> 02:48:40
everyone will appreciate it in the long

02:48:37 --> 02:48:41
run because they'll appreciate kind of

02:48:39 --> 02:48:43
being guided through this process of

02:48:40 --> 02:48:44
studying with friends so yeah studying

02:48:42 --> 02:48:46
with friends is great and I'd really

02:48:43 --> 02:48:48
recommend you give it a try

alrighty so thank you so much for

02:48:47 --> 02:48:51
watching the videos in this class I'm

02:48:49 --> 02:48:53
not sure how many of them you watched

02:48:50 --> 02:48:54
but thank you for enrolling the class

02:48:52 --> 02:48:56
anyway I really hope you found something

02:48:53 --> 02:48:57
at least something in here that you can

02:48:55 --> 02:48:59
take away to hopefully apply to your own

02:48:57 --> 02:49:00
life to your own studying to make the

02:48:58 --> 02:49:02
studying a little bit more efficient and

02:49:00 --> 02:49:03
hopefully a little bit more enjoyable so

02:49:01 --> 02:49:05
that we can you know enjoy the time that

02:49:02 --> 02:49:07
we're spending studying but that we can

02:49:04 --> 02:49:09
also free up our time to do other things

02:49:06 --> 02:49:10
that are more interesting so yeah um if

02:49:08 --> 02:49:12
you have any comments any questions or

02:49:09 --> 02:49:13
anything please do leave a comment in

02:49:11 --> 02:49:15
the discussion section down below and I

02:49:12 --> 02:49:16
will be replying to stuff you can

02:49:14 --> 02:49:17
definitely check out my YouTube channel

02:49:15 --> 02:49:19
for more study tips including some

02:49:16 --> 02:49:20
content that's not in this class there's

02:49:18 --> 02:49:21
quite a few things on on YouTube for

02:49:19 --> 02:49:23
that and if you're interested in

02:49:21 --> 02:49:25
specific apps to help with studying I've

02:49:22 --> 02:49:27
got loads of stuff on how I use notion

02:49:24 --> 02:49:29
Google Sheets and key and a few other

02:49:26 --> 02:49:31
different apps to kind of maximize the

02:49:28 --> 02:49:33
fficiency and the enjoyableness of my

02:49:30 --> 02:49:35
studying so yeah thank you again for

02:49:32 --> 02:49:36
watching this class and all the best

02:49:34 --> 02:49:39
with your studying and see you sometime

soon bye
